=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a grid S with H rows and W columns consisting of 1, 2, 3, and ?. The character at the i-th row and j-th column is S_{i,j}.
By replacing each ? in S with 1, 2, or 3, we can obtain 3^q different grids, where q is the number of ?. Among these grids, how many satisfy the following condition? Print the count modulo 998244353.

- Any two adjacent (edge-sharing) cells contain different digits.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
H W
S_{1,1}S_{1,2}\ldots S_{1,W}
S_{2,1}S_{2,2}\ldots S_{2,W}
\vdots
S_{H,1}S_{H,2}\ldots S_{H,W}

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq H, W
- H \times W \leq 200
- H and W are integers.
- S is a grid with H rows and W columns consisting of 1, 2, 3, and ?.

Sample Input 1

2 2
1?
??

Sample Output 1

6

Among the grids obtained by replacing each ? in S with 1, 2, or 3, the following six grids satisfy the condition.
12  12  12  13  13  13
21  23  31  21  31  32

Sample Input 2

2 3
123
3?1

Sample Output 2

0

None of the grids obtained by replacing ? satisfies the condition.

Sample Input 3

8 8
3?1?????
???1????
??????2?
????????
????????
????13??
??13?1??
????????

Sample Output 3

779135038

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's write code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().strip().split()
    if not data: return
    H = int(data[0]); W = int(data[1])
    S = data[2:2+H]
    if H >= W:
        R, C = H, W
        grid = [[-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch)-49 for ch in row] for row in S]
    else:
        R, C = W, H
        grid = []
        for j in range(W):
            row = []
            for i in range(H):
                ch = S[i][j]
                row.append(-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch)-49)
            grid.append(row)
    # Now R rows, C cols, C <= 14.
    shifts = [2*i for i in range(C)]
    # deltas[c][x][up]
    deltas = []
    for sh in shifts:
        d = [[0]*4 for _ in range(3)]
        for x in range(3):
            for up in range(4):
                d[x][up] = (x - up) << sh
        deltas.append(d)
    not_color = [7 ^ (1 << i) for i in range(3)]  # [6,5,3]
    colors_by_mask = [
        [],
        [0],
        [1],
        [0,1],
        [2],
        [0,2],
        [1,2],
        [0,1,2]
    ]
    init = (1 << (2*C)) - 1  # all digits 3
    dp = {init: 1}
    for r in range(R):
        for c in range(C):
            fixed = grid[r][c]
            fixed_mask = 7 if fixed == -1 else (1 << fixed)
            sh = shifts[c]
            delta = deltas[c]
            ndp = {}
            if c == 0:
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3
                    mask = fixed_mask
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]
                    # if mask == 0 continue
                    for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                        ns = state + delta[x][up]
                        old = ndp.get(ns)
                        if old is None:
                            ndp[ns] = val
                        else:
                            s = old + val
                            if s >= MOD:
                                s -= MOD
                            ndp[ns] = s
            else:
                left_sh = shifts[c-1]
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    left = (state >> left_sh) & 3
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3
                    mask = fixed_mask
                    mask &= not_color[left]
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]
                    for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                        ns = state + delta[x][up]
                        old = ndp.get(ns)
                        if old is None:
                            ndp[ns] = val
                        else:
                            s = old + val
                            if s >= MOD:
                                s -= MOD
                            ndp[ns] = s
            dp = ndp
    ans = sum(dp.values()) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Another subtlety: The DP state does not explicitly store the break position c; it is implicit in the scan step. The same integer state may appear at different c with different meaning? Let's draft final code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    H = int(data[0]); W = int(data[1])
    S = data[2:2+H]

    if H >= W:
        R, C = H, W
        grid = []
        for row in S:
            grid.append([-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch) - 49 for ch in row])
    else:
        R, C = W, H
        grid = []
        for j in range(W):
            row = []
            for i in range(H):
                ch = S[i][j]
                row.append(-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch) - 49)
            grid.append(row)

    shifts = [2 * i for i in range(C)]
    deltas = []
    for sh in shifts:
        d = [[0] * 4 for _ in range(3)]
        for x in range(3):
            for up in range(4):
                d[x][up] = (x - up) << sh
        deltas.append(d)

    not_color = [7 ^ (1 << i) for i in range(3)]
    colors_by_mask = [
        [],
        [0],
        [1],
        [0, 1],
        [2],
        [0, 2],
        [1, 2],
        [0, 1, 2],
    ]

    init = (1 << (2 * C)) - 1
    dp = {init: 1}
    mod = MOD

    for r in range(R):
        for c in range(C):
            fixed = grid[r][c]
            fixed_mask = 7 if fixed == -1 else (1 << fixed)
            sh = shifts[c]
            delta = deltas[c]
            ndp = {}

            if c == 0:
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3
                    mask = fixed_mask
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]
                    if mask:
                        for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                            ns = state + delta[x][up]
                            old = ndp.get(ns)
                            if old is None:
                                ndp[ns] = val
                            else:
                                s = old + val
                                if s >= mod:
                                    s -= mod
                                if s:
                                    ndp[ns] = s
                                else:
                                    del ndp[ns]
            else:
                left_sh = shifts[c - 1]
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    left = (state >> left_sh) & 3
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3
                    mask = fixed_mask
                    mask &= not_color[left]
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]
                    if mask:
                        for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                            ns = state + delta[x][up]
                            old = ndp.get(ns)
                            if old is None:
                                ndp[ns] = val
                            else:
                                s = old + val
                                if s >= mod:
                                    s -= mod
                                if s:
                                    ndp[ns] = s
                                else:
                                    del ndp[ns]
            dp = ndp
            if not dp:
                # Can print 0 and return early
                print(0)
                return

    ans = sum(dp.values()) % mod
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Need consider early return if dp empty: Is it always safe? If no partial colorings, no full colorings. Yes. But if dp empty after some cell, answer 0. We can return. Good. However, if dp empty due to modulo zero? We delete zero states, so dp empty means no partial colorings modulo? Wait counts modulo could be zero for all states even if there are partial colorings whose count is multiple of MOD. Is that possible? We are counting modulo MOD. If all partial colorings count is 0 modulo MOD, then dp could be empty after deleting zero states. But there might be full colorings whose count modulo MOD is nonzero? Let's think. The DP stores counts modulo MOD. If at an intermediate cell, the number of partial colorings for every state is 0 modulo MOD, then for any completion, the total will be 0 modulo MOD because future transitions are linear and multiply/add by 1. So answer modulo MOD is 0. Thus early return is safe. But wait, if dp empty because all state counts are 0 mod MOD, then indeed no contribution modulo MOD to any final coloring. So answer 0 mod MOD. Good. But is it possible that some state count is 0 mod MOD but another state count nonzero, and we delete zeros; dp not empty. Fine. Early return if dp empty safe.

However, there is a subtlety: We delete zero states during ndp construction. If a state's count becomes 0 mod MOD, we remove it. If all states become 0, dp empty. That means sum of counts for all partial colorings is 0 mod MOD. Future transitions won't create nonzero from zero. So answer 0. Safe.

But wait, if dp empty after a cell, could there be a state with count 0 mod MOD that we deleted, but later when combined with future choices, the count could become nonzero? No, linearity: 0 * anything = 0. So no.

But let's verify with binary representation: state digit c is 3 (binary 11). delta = -1 * 4^c = - (1 << sh). Subtracting 1<<sh from a number with bit sh and sh+1 set? Wait digit 3 at position c means bits 2c and 2c+1 are 1. Subtracting 1<<2c (which is 4^c) from 3*4^c yields 2*4^c, digit 2. No borrow because bit 2c is 1. Subtracting 2<<sh from 3*4^c yields 1*4^c. In binary, 1 at bit sh plus 1 at bit sh = 2 at bit sh, which is carry to bit sh+1? But in base 4, digit 2 is binary 10 at bits sh+1, sh. The integer addition 1<<sh + 1<<sh = 2<<sh, which sets bit sh+1 and clears bit sh. That's exactly digit 2, no carry beyond digit because 2<4. Good. If up=0, delta=2<<sh, digit 0 ->2. No carry. If up=2, delta=+? x=2, up=0 delta=2<<sh, digit0 ->2. Good. If up=1, x=0 delta=-1<<sh, digit1 ->0. No borrow. Good.

If up=3, x=0..2, delta=-3,-2,-1<<sh, digit c=3, no borrow. So no borrow. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python

import sys

MOD = 998244353

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    H = int(data[0])
    W = int(data[1])
    S = data[2:2 + H]

    # Use the smaller dimension as the frontier width.
    if H >= W:
        R, C = H, W
        grid = []
        for row in S:
            grid.append([-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch) - 49 for ch in row])
    else:
        R, C = W, H
        grid = []
        for j in range(W):
            row = []
            for i in range(H):
                ch = S[i][j]
                row.append(-1 if ch == '?' else ord(ch) - 49)
            grid.append(row)

    shifts = [2 * i for i in range(C)]

    # delta[c][color][up] = (color - up) << (2*c)
    deltas = []
    for sh in shifts:
        d = [[0] * 4 for _ in range(3)]
        for x in range(3):
            for up in range(4):
                d[x][up] = (x - up) << sh
        deltas.append(d)

    not_color = [7 ^ (1 << i) for i in range(3)]
    colors_by_mask = [
        [],
        [0],
        [1],
        [0, 1],
        [2],
        [0, 2],
        [1, 2],
        [0, 1, 2],
    ]

    # Initial frontier: all cells are "no previous row" sentinel 3.
    init = (1 << (2 * C)) - 1
    dp = {init: 1}
    mod = MOD

    for r in range(R):
        for c in range(C):
            fixed = grid[r][c]
            fixed_mask = 7 if fixed == -1 else (1 << fixed)

            sh = shifts[c]
            delta = deltas[c]
            ndp = {}

            if c == 0:
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3
                    mask = fixed_mask
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]

                    if mask:
                        for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                            ns = state + delta[x][up]
                            old = ndp.get(ns)
                            if old is None:
                                ndp[ns] = val
                            else:
                                s = old + val
                                if s >= mod:
                                    s -= mod
                                if s:
                                    ndp[ns] = s
                                else:
                                    del ndp[ns]
            else:
                left_sh = shifts[c - 1]
                for state, val in dp.items():
                    left = (state >> left_sh) & 3
                    up = (state >> sh) & 3

                    mask = fixed_mask
                    mask &= not_color[left]
                    if up != 3:
                        mask &= not_color[up]

                    if mask:
                        for x in colors_by_mask[mask]:
                            ns = state + delta[x][up]
                            old = ndp.get(ns)
                            if old is None:
                                ndp[ns] = val
                            else:
                                s = old + val
                                if s >= mod:
                                    s -= mod
                                if s:
                                    ndp[ns] = s
                                else:
                                    del ndp[ns]

            dp = ndp
            if not dp:
                print(0)
                return

    print(sum(dp.values()) % mod)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
